Then first sum i=0..n-d-1 = H_{n-d}; second i=d..n-1 = H_n - H_d. Total sum= N. So C_d = H_{n-d} - (H_n - H_d) = H_{n-d} + H_d - N. Nice! For d>0. Great. Directed D_d = (K_d + C_d)/2, D_{-d}=(K_d - C_d)/2. Objective max_d max(D_d,D_-d) = (K_d + |C_d|)/2. For linear shifts, max over d>0 of (K_d+|C_d|)/2. D_0=0.

This is promising. K_d (disagreements) update maybe simple; C_d depends on prefix sums of h and can be updated under swap in O(n) but maybe even O(1) for range? def apply_swap(k, c, a, i, j):
    ai = a[i]; aj = a[j]
    if i+1 < n? need n global or len(k)
    # lower for i
    k[1:n-i] += ai * a[i+1:n]  # slice length n-i-1? If i=0, k[1:n] length n-1; a[1:n] length n-1. Good. d=1..n-1-i.
    if i > 0: k[1:i+1] += ai * a[i-1::-1]
    # lower for j
    k[1:n-j] += aj * a[j+1:n]
    if j > 0: k[1:j+1] += aj * a[j-1::-1]
    d = abs(i-j)
    k[d] += -2 * ai * aj  # correction
    # C slices
    # d > i: if i+1 <= n-1: k? c[i+1:n] -=1
    # d > j: c[j+1:n] +=1
    # d < n-i: c[1:n-i] -=1
    # d < n-j: c[1:n-j] +=1
    if i + 1 < n: c[i+1:n] -= 1
    if j + 1 < n: c[j+1:n] += 1
    if n - i > 1: c[1:n-i] -= 1
    if n - j > 1: c[1:n-j] += 1
    # flip a
    a[i] = -ai; a[j] = -aj

Need ensure slices for K: If n=2,i=0: k[1:2] length1; a[1:2] length1. k[1:i+1] none. Good. If i=n-1: k[1:n-(n-1)=1] empty; upper k[1:n] length n-1; a[n-2::-1]. Good.
C slices: For i=0: c[1:n] -=1? d>0 yes all d >0. For n-i = n, c[1:n] -1? d<n yes all d <n. That matches? Let's formalize. Choose n=2m maybe. Let p = n-1 (odd) if prime ≡? For p=511, cyclic universe of size p. We have one extra index to make length n. If we take indices 0..p-1 as cyclic, and index p as additional (maybe duplicate something). For pairs among 0..p-1, ordered linear difference t=z-p? Actually z,y in [0,p-1]. Each ordered pair (one p_pos, zero z) with z - y = t in [-(p-1), p-1] directly. This is not modulo; it's the actual signed difference. For a fixed modulo residue r, the linear signed differences that map to r are r if 0≤r≤p-1 or r-p (negative) if r-p within. For each modulo ordered pair (one y, zero z), the linear t = z-y if z≥y else z-y (negative); this is exactly the signed representative in [-(p-1),p-1]. So the multiset of signed differences has for residue r>0 count A_r (for pairs where z-y=r?) plus maybe? In the cyclic universe, D_p(r) counts pairs (y, z) with z ≡ y+r mod p. These correspond to two signed linear differences: r (when z=y+r <p) and r-(p) (when z<y? Actually if z = y+r mod p, there are two possibilities: if y+r<p, t=r; if y+r≥p, z=y+r-p, t=r-p = -(p-r). Thus the cyclic count for residue r is split into linear positive r and negative p-r. For residue r, pairs with no wrap go to +r; wrap go to -(p-r). For residue p-r similarly wrap to -(p-r) and no wrap to +(p-r). The signed count for +t (t>0) equals number of cyclic pairs with residue t and no wrap. The signed count for -t equals number with residue t and wrap plus? Symmetric. We need both directions. If cyclic D_p(r)=constant C_d for all r, then for each t>0, the total C_d(+t)+C_d(-t) =? We can implement a deterministic greedy "maximum reduction" local search: starting from some initial, while possible, find a swap that reduces current max. But choosing random may take long. We can accelerate by using current D_plus/minus arrays to target lags.

Let's understand how a swap affects a particular lag. Suppose worst is D_d_plus (positive lag, 1 at i, 0 at i+d). To reduce it, flipping i to 0 or i+d to 1 can remove contributions; but must maintain balance by opposite swap elsewhere. If we choose i as the 1 and j as the 0 at i+d? Swapping them would make h_i=0,h_j=1, reducing D_d_plus by 1 for that pair, but creates pairs? If i and j distance d, actual pair changes from 10 to 01, so D_plus at d decreases by1, D_minus at d increases by1. If both directions have same max, this doesn't reduce max unless one had slack. It may shift load. If one direction is worst and other not, good. We can target direction with more headroom? However max over both. If K_d fixed? A swap of adjacent 10 to 01 increases imbalance but not K? For distance d, K remains same, C changes. Could reduce max if C_d was positive and K+C was max, making C negative maybe still |C|. If C_d > M - K_d? Hmm.

Another move: choose a 1 in bad pair and swap with a 0 not at distance d to convert 10 to 00, reducing D_d. Or swap the 0 at i+d to 1 (with a 1 elsewhere) converts to 11, reducing D_d. Need choose j to minimize other increases. We can search locally among candidate i (bad positions) and j (zeros/ones). For each candidate, apply update (exact) and check global max. We can try many. This is a better heuristic than random.

Implementation targeted:
- Compute Dplus = (K+C)//2, Dminus=(K-C)//2. Arrays length n (indices 1..n-1). Find max value cur. Lag d and sign: if Dplus[d] >= Dminus[d]: worst positive. Positive lag d: bad pairs are i in ones with zero at i+d (i=0..n-d-1). We can collect? Need random bad i. Could find by scanning h? To avoid O(n) per step maybe okay. For each trial, we can randomly sample i from ones and check h[i+d]==0; or precompute lists for each lag? Too memory. O(n) scan per targeted trial might be heavy but okay if fewer.
- Candidate set for positive lag d: positions i in ones and i+d< n and zero at i+d. If choose i (one) to swap with a zero j, we reduce this contribution if j not? Actually flipping i to 0 removes it. Need j zero. We can choose j random zero, maybe with preference not create new contributions at same lag: If j+d? If j one after flip? It will create contribution at j if zero at j+d. Prefer j with h[j+d]=1 (no new bad) or j+d out? Similarly for negative.
- For negative lag d (D_-d): bad pairs i in ones with zero at i-d (zero before one). Swapping i to 0 or zero before to1? D_-d count pairs where zero at i-d, one at i. To reduce, swap i one with zero? If swap i with zero before? etc.

But random global swaps may be enough if we run many. Targeted can improve stagnation.

Could also use "steepest descent" with candidate generation: For current worst lag, generate up to C candidates i bad, j zeros (or ones?) and evaluate using apply/revert. Evaluate maybe 1000 candidates per iteration, accept best improvement. This is more expensive but can escape. We can implement a cycle:
  - Compute max cur, count of max lags.
  - Choose worst lag/sign.
  - Generate candidate swaps around bad pairs:
     * If sign +: for i bad (h_i=1,h_{i+d}=0). Candidate j from zeros: maybe the zero at i+d (swap the pair) and random zeros. For each j, apply_swap, cur_try. Track best. Revert.
     * If sign -: bad i where h_i=1,h_{i-d}=0. Candidate j zeros similarly.
  - Accept move with lowest cur_try (if <cur). If none, random hill climb/anneal.
But O(candidates * n) per improvement; can be okay for 1000 candidates *512 ~0.5M ops, a few hundred times.

We also can use "batch" local search: for each active lag above threshold, random fix.

Maybe simpler: Use simulated annealing with current K/C; after maintaining best, if stall, do a "kick": randomly accept a move increasing max by up to L for many steps to perturb, then hill climb. This is standard.

Optimization loop candidate:
def hill_climb(k,c,a,ones,zeros,cur,global_best,timeout, rng, max_tries, anneal=False):
   accepted=0; stall=0; start=time.time()
   T=... 
   while time.time()-start < timeout and ...:
      i=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]; j=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]
      apply_swap(k,c,a,i,j)
      newcur=max_count(k,c)
      do_accept = newcur < cur
      if anneal and not do_accept and newcur <= cur + 2:
          delta = newcur - cur
          # accept prob exp(-delta/T)
          do_accept = rng.random() < math.exp(-delta/T)
      if do_accept:
         cur = newcur
         # swap in lists
         idx_i = ones.index(i)? We need O(1) update. If we choose by random index p, we know p. For random proposal, use p_i = rng.randrange(len(ones)), p_j=rng.randrange(len(zeros)); i=ones[p_i]; j=zeros[p_j]. On accept: ones[p_i]=j; zeros[p_j]=i.
         if newcur < global_best_count: update global
         stall=0; accepted++.
      else:
         apply_swap(k,c,a,j,i) # revert
         stall+=1
      if anneal and every 1000 cooling: T*=0.99 or schedule
      if stall > limit: break or kick
   return cur, list state? We need k,c,a,ones,zeros lists. We can mutate in place.

Let's design optimize_state:
Inputs: k,c,a,ones,zeros, timeout, rng, target_count? target maybe floor((best_c5)*n/2). But target optional.
Phases:
1. Deadline = start + timeout.
2. cur = max_count.
3. best_cur=cur; best_a=a.copy()
4. Try greedy random swaps until stall_limit reached or time.
   stall_limit maybe 20000. In greedy, accept only if newcur < cur (strict global max). Update best if cur<best_cur.
   If no cur improvement for stall_limit, go to anneal.
5. Annealing: temperature schedule from e.g. 3 down to 0.1. For many trials:
   - random swap
   - compute newcur
   - if newcur < cur: accept
   - elif newcur <= cur + 2: accept with probability exp(-(newcur-cur)/T)
   - else reject
   - update best if newcur<best_cur
   - Cool T *= cooling every e.g. 2000 trials.
   - If T < 0.05, treat as greedy and maybe restart with kick (accept a bad move) if time.
6. Kick: If stuck and time, perform a sequence of random moves ignoring objective (or accept up to +2) to perturb, then greedy. But random proposals with acceptance maybe enough.

Need manage lists when accepting. Random proposal by indices p_i,p_j. For accepted, swap list entries. For rejected, no list change. For kick accept worse, same list update.

Annealing can accept moves increasing cur; if later rejected, revert. Good. But if cur increases a lot, best preserved.

Continuous optimization approach with n=256/512:
Objective F(h)=max_l L_l(h), L_l=∑ h_i (1-h_{i+l}). We can use smooth max S_beta = logsumexp(beta*(L_l - maxL)) / beta + maxL. Gradient:
For each l, ∂L_l/∂h_i:
- source term: (1-h_{i+l}) if i+l valid
- target term: -h_{i-l} if i-l valid? Because L_l includes h_{i-l}(1-h_i) => derivative -h_{i-l}.
For a vector w_l (softmax weights over l), gradient = ∑ w_l ∂L_l/∂h. We can compute weights exponentially. We need sum constraint. Could do projected gradient: start h continuous feasible. Iterate: compute L array length 2n-1 (or only nonzero lags). Use logsumexp stable. Compute weights. Compute grad vector by accumulating contributions from lags. Naively O(n^2) per iteration if loop lags. For n=256, maybe okay if few iterations. But 1300s enough for more.

Can compute gradient efficiently with convolutions:
L_t = ∑ h_i (1-h_{i+t}). Derivative w.r.t h_p:
= ∑_t w_t [ (1-h_{p+t}) I(p+t valid) - h_{p-t} I(p-t valid) ]
= ∑_t w_t I(p+t valid) - ∑_t w_t h_{p+t} I - ∑_t w_t h_{p-t}I
First term: ∑ valid t w_t. For t from max(-p,n-1-p) to ... This is window sum of w over lags where p+t in range. The second term: cross-correlation of w with h: ∑ q w_q h_{p+q} (q valid). Third: ∑ q w_q h_{p-q}. Can compute via np.correlate O(n^2) direct or FFT. For n small, maybe direct loops okay. But implementing may be error. We can also use finite differences? Not.

Max info function:
def d_max_info(D):
   m = float(np.max(D))
   # count of entries equal to m within tiny eps
   # For binary, exact. For continuous, eps 1e-12
   cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(np.abs(D - m) < 1e-12))
   return m, cnt

 def enforce_sum(h, target):
    h = np.clip(h,0,1)
    # make target integer n/2. Use long double? 
    s = h.sum(dtype=np.float64)
    diff = float(target) - s
    # Try iterate: if diff > 0, add to indices with largest 1-h; if diff<0 subtract from largest h.
    for _ in range(100):
       if abs(diff) <= 1e-15: break
       if diff >0:
          room = 1.0 - h
          # choose idx with max room, add min(room[idx], diff)
          idx = int(np.argmax(room)); add = min(room[idx], diff); h[idx]+=add; diff -= add
       else:
          idx=int(np.argmax(h)); sub=min(h[idx], -diff); h[idx]-=sub; diff += sub
       # This may not make float exact due round.
    # final: compute s again; if diff !=0, add diff to one idx with room; due rounding maybe.
    # Or set one element: choose idx interior, old=h[idx], h[idx]=target - (h.sum() - old); clip; repeat maybe.
    # To get exact float sum? Target is integer. We can set h[idx] = np.longdouble(target) - np.longdouble(np.sum(h, dtype=np.longdouble) + old? ) then cast. But sum of cast values may not exact.
    # In practice isclose.

Optimize function:
def optimize_state(h, D, n, rng, timeout, use_binary_phase=True, anneal_start=2.0, anneal_end=0.1):
   h = h.copy(); D=D.copy()
   target = n/2
   h = enforce_sum(h)
   D = recompute? If enforce changes, need recompute D. But initial should exact. After binary phase exact. Continuous maybe. We'll not enforce inside except initial.
   cur_m, cur_cnt = max_info(D)
   best_m=cur_m; best_h=h.copy()
   start=time.time(); deadline=start+timeout
   # If h is nearly binary:
   max_dev = max(abs(h-round(h))) maybe.
   if use_binary_phase and max_dev < 1e-8:
       zeros=[... h<0.5]; ones=[... >0.5]
       T=anneal_start; no_improve=0; trial=0
       while time < deadline- maybe reserve:
          if no_improve > 50000: break? But maybe continue anneal.
          # generate p from zeros q from ones
          p_idx=rng.randint(len(zeros)); q_idx=rng.randint(len(ones)); p=zeros[p_idx]; q=ones[q_idx]
          apply_exchange(h,D,p,q,1.0)
          nm,nc=max_info(D)
          delta = nm - cur_m
          accept=False
          if delta < -1e-12: accept=True
          elif abs(delta) <=1e-12:
              if nc < cur_cnt: accept=True
              # optional small neutral probability
              elif rng.random() < 0.02: accept=True
          else:
              # allow worse up to max_delta using temperature
              if delta < 5.0:
                 # T in count units
                 if rng.random() < math.exp(-delta / max(T,1e-9)):
                    accept=True
          if accept:
             cur_m=nm; cur_cnt=nc
             if nm < best_m - 1e-12:
                best_m=nm; best_h=h.copy()
             # update lists
             zeros[p_idx]=q; ones[q_idx]=p
             no_improve=0
          else:
             # revert
             apply_exchange(h,D,q,p,1.0) # increase q (old zero? after swap q is one? Wait after forward: p was zero -> one, q was one -> zero. To revert, increase q by1, decrease p by1. apply_exchange(h,D,q,p,1.0) does increase q, decrease p. Correct.
             no_improve +=1
          trial +=1
          if trial % 2000 ==0:
             T = max(anneal_end, T * 0.98)
             if no_improve > 30000:
                T = min(3.0, T + 0.5) # reheat
                no_improve = 0
       # after binary phase, if time left
   # Continuous phase: (if h not exactly binary or we want polish)
   if time < deadline-5:
       # determine starting T for continuous maybe 0.5 (count units) but delta small.
       # We can do exchanges with variable delta.
       T_cont = 1.0
       no_prog=0
       try_count=0
       while time < deadline-2:
          # choose p,q. To speed, sample arrays maybe.
          p=rng.randint(n); q=rng.randint(n)
          if p==q: continue
          maxp=1.0-h[p]; maxq=h[q]
          if maxp < 1e-12 or maxq < 1e-12: continue
          # choose delta: bias to full transfer, but allow smaller
          u = rng.random()
          if u < 0.5:
              delta = min(maxp,maxq) # full
          else:
              delta = min(maxp,maxq) * rng.uniform(0.05, 1.0)
          # optional if delta very small <1e-6 skip unless many fails? skip
          if delta < 1e-8: continue
          apply_exchange(h,D,p,q,delta)
          nm,nc = max_info(D)
          delta_obj = nm - cur_m
          accept=False
          if delta_obj < -1e-12: accept=True
          elif abs(delta_obj)<=1e-12:
              if nc < cur_cnt: accept=True
              elif rng.random() < 0.05: accept=True
          else:
              if delta_obj < 1.0: # count units
                 if rng.random() < math.exp(-delta_obj / max(T_cont,1e-9)): accept=True
          if accept:
             cur_m=nm; cur_cnt=nc
             if nm < best_m - 1e-12:
                best_m=nm; best_h=h.copy()
             no_prog=0
             T_cont = max(0.05, T_cont * (0.999 if delta_obj <0 else 1.0))? Simpler cool slowly.
          else:
             apply_exchange(h,D,q,p,delta) # revert (increase q, decrease p by delta)
             no_prog +=1
          try_count +=1
          if try_count % 5000 ==0:
             T_cont = max(0.05, T_cont*0.97)
             if no_prog > 20000:
                T_cont = min(2.0, T_cont+0.5)
                no_prog=0
   return best_h, best_m

Since the final code must be reliable and not too long. Need balance. We can implement a task processor:
```
def process_tasks(tasks, best_h, best_c5, best_n, start_time, budget_s):
   for n,h_init,timeout in tasks:
       if remaining()<10: break
       timeout = min(timeout, remaining()-10)
       if timeout < 15: continue
       h_opt,m_opt=optimize_state(h_init,...,timeout)
       c=m_opt*2.0/n
       h_final=enforce_sum(h_opt)
       c=compute_c5(h_final)
       if c<best_c5: update
   return
```

Let's define our D convention:
offset = n-1
D[idx = offset + t] = ∑_{i=0}^{n-1-t} h[i] * (1 - h[i+t]) for t ≥0? For t negative, i from -t to n-1: ∑ h[i]*(1-h[i+t]) valid. Equivalent for all t: ∑_{i: 0≤i<n,0≤i+t<n} h[i]*(1-h[i+t]).
Initial compute directly:
```
D = np.zeros(2*n-1)
for t in range(-(n-1), n):
    idx=offset+t
    if t>=0:
        D[idx] = np.dot(h[:n-t], 1-h[t:])
    else:
        s=-t
        D[idx] = np.dot(h[s:], 1-h[:n-s])
```
This O(n^2) with vector dot per t; n=512, 1023*avg256=262k C ops per init, negligible. For many inits maybe 10*512^2=2.6M, fine. For n=1024 ~1M, 10 inits 10M, fine.
```
def initial_d(h):
    n=len(h); off=n-1; D=np.zeros(2*n-1,float)
    for t in range(-off, off+1):
       idx=off+t
       if t==0:
           D[idx]=np.dot(h,1-h) # could sum h*(1-h)
       elif t>0:
           D[idx]=np.dot(h[:n-t], 1-h[t:])
       else:
           s=-t
           D[idx]=np.dot(h[s:], 1-h[:n-s])
    return D
```

```
def targeted_binary_improve(h,D,n,ones,zeros,cur_m,cur_cnt,rng, num_trials=200):
    # assume h binary-ish
    # if no list positions? return False
    if not ones or not zeros: return False
    best_nm=cur_m; best_nc=cur_cnt+1; best_p=best_q=-1
    # choose 2 worst lags
    maxD = D.max()
    worst_indices = np.where(np.abs(D - maxD) < 1e-12)[0]
    # maybe sample up to 3 worst
    w_indices = rng.choice(worst_indices, size=min(3,len), replace=False)
    for w in w_indices:
       t=w-(n-1)
       bad_p=[]; bad_q=[]
       if t>=0:
          if D[w] > 0:
             # contributions h[i]=1,h[i+t]=0
             # sample from ones and check target zero
             for _ in range(min(30,len(ones))):
                q=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]
                if q+t<n and h[q+t] < 0.5:
                   bad_p.append(q+t); bad_q.append(q)
       else:
          s=-t
          # D_t: source i >=s, h[i]=1, h[i-s]=0
          for _ in range(min(30,len(ones))):
             q=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]
             if q>=s and h[q-s] < 0.5:
                bad_p.append(q-s); bad_q.append(q)
       if not bad_p: continue
       # candidates
       for idx in range(min(10,len(bad_p))):
          p=bad_p[idx]; q=bad_q[idx]
          # pair swap
          evaluate(p,q)
          # p (zero) swap with random one (including maybe)
          for r in range(5):
             rr=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]? Actually to flip zero p to one, choose q random one. evaluate(p,rr)
          # q (one) swap with random zero (flip q to zero)
          for r in range(5):
             rr=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]; evaluate(q,rr)
    # after all, if best improvement, apply
```

Implementation details for `enforce_sum`:
```
def enforce_sum(h, target):
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0).astype(float, copy=True)
    # Use a loop adding/subtracting from largest room to reach target.
    for _ in range(100):
        s = h.sum()
        diff = target - s
        if abs(diff) < 1e-13:
            break
        if diff > 0:
            room = 1.0 - h
            idx = int(np.argmax(room))
            add = min(room[idx], diff)
            h[idx] += add
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(h))
            sub = min(h[idx], -diff)
            h[idx] -= sub
    # If still not close, distribute over all
    diff = target - h.sum()
    if abs(diff) > 1e-12:
       if diff >0:
          mask = h < 1-1e-12
          if mask.any(): h[mask] += diff/np.count_nonzero(mask)
       else:
          mask = h > 1e-12
          if mask.any(): h[mask] += diff/np.count_nonzero(mask)
       h = np.clip(h,0,1)
    return h
```
This may not make exact but close. For binary initial, return unchanged (sum integer exact). For continuous, evaluator tolerance likely.

But if we use enforce on continuous after local search, it may change D slightly; final compute_c5 after enforce. Good. However local search state D would be inconsistent if we use best_h after random; final compute exact. Fine.

Can D update with continuous and enforce not preserve exactly? We only enforce at start and final, not during search. If initial sum off, exchange preserves whatever sum? If initial sum not target, final might not. Enforce before search ensures target (within float). During search exchange preserves (numerically close). Good.

Now, how to generate `Thue-Morse` efficiently for n power of two:
```
def thue_morse_h(n):
   h = np.zeros(n)
   for i in range(n): h[i]=1.0 if bin(i).count('1')%2==0 else 0.0
   # if n not power? n power. If not, enforce sum by threshold largest? all balanced if power of2.
```

Anti-symmetric init from values first_half:
```
def antisym_from_first(first):
   return np.concatenate([first, 1-first])
```
Need length even.

Random binary:
```
def random_binary(n,rng):
   h=np.zeros(n); idx=rng.choice(n,n//2,replace=False); h[idx]=1; return h
```

Sample parent:
```
def resample_values(vals, n):
   if len(vals)==n: return vals.copy()
   x_old=np.linspace(0,2,len(vals),endpoint=False); x_new=np.linspace(0,2,n,endpoint=False)
   return np.interp(x_new,x_old,vals)
```
Threshold top target:
```
def threshold_top_half(vals, n, rng=None, noise=0.0):
   v=vals.astype(float).copy()
   if noise>0: v += rng.normal(0,noise,v.size)
   target=n//2
   # sort descending
   idx=np.argsort(v, kind='stable')[::-1][:target]
   h=np.zeros(n); h[idx]=1
   return h
```
For n=256 parent length 256, noise threshold variants.

```
def run(seed=42,budget_s=1300,**kwargs):
  np.random.seed(seed)
  rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
  t0=time.time()
  def time_left? cannot closure; use helper? We can compute inline elapsed. No closures rule: "Make all helper functions top level, no closures or lambdas". Inside run, cannot define nested functions. So we must avoid nested def. We can compute remaining = budget_s - (time.time()-start_time). OK.
  best_h=None; best_c5=inf; best_n=1
  # parent eval
  parent=None; parent_n=0
  try:
     if len(initial_h_values)>0: parent=np.asarray(initial_h_values,dtype=float) # does this count copy? It's runtime not response. OK.
  except: parent=None
  if parent is not None:
     # enforce target = len(parent)//2? Parent sum should target. But if len odd? Could be. enforce.
     h_p=enforce_sum(parent, len(parent)/2.0) # target float
     c=compute_c5(h_p)
     # If len(parent) not power? Use as is? Could return if no better? We'll add.
     if c<best_c5: best_h=h_p.copy(); best_c5=c; best_n=len(parent)
  # tasks: list of (n,h,timeout)
  tasks=[]
  # --- n=256
  n=256
  if remaining > 200:
     if parent is not None:
        hp=resample_values(parent,n)
        tasks.append((n, threshold_top_half(hp,n, rng, noise=0.0), 120))
        tasks.append((n, enforce_sum(hp,n/2), 120)) # continuous parent
        for noise in [0.02,0.05,0.1]:
           tasks.append((n, threshold_top_half(hp,n, rng, noise), 60))
     tasks.append((n, thue_morse_h(n), 120))
     # anti from random or thue first half
     first = thue_morse_h(n//2); tasks.append((n, antisym(first), 60))
     for _ in range(3): tasks.append((n, random_binary(n,rng), 60))
  # --- n=512

     n=512

        hp512=resample_values(parent,n)
        tasks.append((n, threshold_top_half(hp512,n), 150))
        tasks.append((n, enforce_sum(hp512,n/2), 150))
        tasks.append((n, threshold_top_half(hp512,n, rng, 0.03), 90))
     # use best from 256 if available? but tasks processed sequentially; we haven't run 256 yet. Could postpone 512 tasks after 256 processed.
```

Define helper `process_task_list(tasks, best_h,best_c5,best_n, rng, start_time,budget_s)` top-level returning updated. But cannot use closures. It can take start_time,budget_s and use time.time inside. It will loop tasks minimizing timeout to remaining. It needs rng. Returns best tuple and maybe elapsed.

```
def process_tasks(tasks, best_h, best_c5, best_n, rng, start_time, budget_s):
   best_h,best_c5,best_n = best_h,best_c5,best_n
   reserve=5.0
   for task in tasks:
      n,h_init,timeout = task
      remaining = budget_s - (time.time()-start_time)
      if remaining < 20: break
      timeout = min(timeout, remaining - reserve)
      if timeout < 10: break
      h_opt,m_opt = optimize_state(h_init, rng, timeout, start_time?, budget_s?)
      # optimize_state can itself deadline=start+timeout, no budget needed.
      h_opt = enforce_sum(h_opt, n/2.0)
      c = compute_c5(h_opt)
      if c < best_c5 - 1e-12:
         best_h=h_opt.copy(); best_c5=c; best_n=n
   return best_h,best_c5,best_n
```
Need `optimize_state` doesn't know budget? It takes timeout and uses time.time internally. Good.

Let's detail optimize_state to be efficient and robust.

```
def optimize_state(h_init, rng, timeout, binary_mode=True):
    h = np.array(h_init, dtype=float).copy()
    n=h.size; target=n/2.0
    h = enforce_sum(h,target)
    D = initial_d(h)
    cur_m, cur_cnt = get_max_info(D)
    best_m = cur_m
    best_h = h.copy()
    start=time.time(); deadline=start+timeout
    # determine if binary
    if binary_mode:
        hr = np.round(h)
        if np.max(np.abs(h-hr)) < 1e-7:
            h = hr
            # recompute D? enforce maybe exact; D from binary.
            D = initial_d(h) # maybe if h changed tiny? cost negligible
            cur_m,cur_cnt = get_max_info(D)
            best_m=cur_m; best_h=h.copy()
            zeros=[i for i,v in enumerate(h) if v<0.5]
            ones=[i for i,v in enumerate(h) if v>=0.5]
            # binary_search loop
            T=2.0; no_improve=0; trial=0; targeted_every=10000
            while time.time() < deadline - 0.1:
                if no_improve > 30000:
                    # reheat or break? If time left, reheat; else break
                    if T < 3.0: T = min(3.0, T+1.0)
                    no_improve=0
                    if trial % 100000 ==0 and time.time() > deadline-...? 
                # check time every few? time.time each loop may be overhead but okay.
                # maybe every 5000
                if trial % 5000 ==0 and time.time() > deadline: break
                pi = rng.randint(len(zeros)); oi = rng.randint(len(ones)); p=zeros[pi]; q=ones[oi]
                apply_exchange(h,D,p,q,1.0)
                nm,nc=get_max_info(D)
                diff=nm-cur_m
                accept=False
                if diff < -1e-12: accept=True
                elif abs(diff) < 1e-12:
                    if nc < cur_cnt: accept=True
                    elif rng.random() < 0.03: accept=True
                else:
                    if diff < 5.0:
                       # temperature in count units
                        if rng.random() < math.exp(-diff / max(T,1e-12)): accept=True
                if accept:
                    cur_m=nm; cur_cnt=nc
                    if nm < best_m - 1e-12:
                       best_m=nm; best_h=h.copy()
                    zeros[pi]=q; ones[oi]=p
                    no_improve=0
                else:
                    apply_exchange(h,D,q,p,1.0)
                    no_improve +=1
                trial +=1
                # cooling
                if trial % 2000 ==0:
                    T = max(0.1, T*0.985)
                    if no_improve > 8000 and time.time() < deadline-30:
                       # targeted
                       if targeted_binary_improve(...):
                          # update best/cur maybe
                          no_improve=0
```
But function call to time.time each iteration can be significant? For 1M iterations, time.time calls 1M maybe ~0.1s? Actually maybe 0.5us, okay. But counting trial and modulo overhead. We can check time every 1000 by maintaining next_check.

```
next_check=0
trial=0
while True:
   trial +=1

```

`targeted_binary_improve` should perhaps not use current best_m? It improves current state. We'll write:
```
def targeted_binary_improve(h,D,n,ones,zeros,cur_m,cur_cnt,rng, attempts=100):
    # returns new_cur_m,new_cur_cnt, accepted_changed
    if len(ones)==0 or len(zeros)==0: return ...
    best_nm=cur_m; best_nc=cur_cnt; best_mv=False; best_p=-1; best_q=-1
    # pick up to 3 current worst lags
    maxD = D.max()
    worst = np.where(np.abs(D - maxD) <= 1e-10)[0]
    if worst.size > 3: worst = rng.choice(worst,3,replace=False)
    for w in worst:
       t = int(w - (n-1))
       # candidate pairs list length maybe 30
       cand=[]
       # sample bad pairs from contributions
       if t >=0:
          for _ in range(30):
             q=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]
             if q + t < n and h[q+t] < 0.5:
                cand.append((q+t, q))
       else:
          s=-t
          for _ in range(30):
             q=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]
             if q >= s and h[q-s] < 0.5:
                cand.append((q-s, q))
       for p,q in cand[:10]:
          # evaluate pair swap
          nm,nc=eval_swap_revert(h,D,p,q,n)
          update best
          # p zero with random ones (flip p to1)
          for _ in range(3):
             rr=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]
             if rr != p:
                nm,nc=eval_swap_revert(h,D,p,rr,n)
                update
          # q one with random zeros (flip q to0)
          for _ in range(3):
             rr=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]
             if rr != q:
                nm,nc=eval_swap_revert(h,D,rr,q,n)
                update
    if best_mv:
       apply_exchange(h,D,best_p,best_q,1.0)
       return best_nm, best_nc, True
    return cur_m,cur_cnt,False
```
But `eval_swap_revert` will apply_exchange with delta=1, assuming p zero,q one. In candidate p may be equal q? skip. Need ensure p from zeros, q from ones; for bad pair t>=0 q one, p=target zero. OK. Random rr from ones/zeros. If p is zero and rr one. Good.
If h not exactly binary (but binary phase condition), values are 0/1.

Continuous phase code:
```
def continuous_phase(h,D,n,rng,deadline,cur_m,cur_cnt,best_m,best_h):
    T=0.3
    no_imp=0; trial=0; next_check=2000
    # Build inc/dec lists maybe
    inc = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] < 1-1e-9]
    dec = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] > 1e-9]
    rebuild_every=2000
    while trial<... time:
       if next_check due time
       if trial % rebuild_every ==0:
          inc = ...; dec=...
          if not inc or not dec: break
       p=inc[rng.randint(len(inc))]; q=dec[rng.randint(len(dec))]; if p==q continue
       maxp=1-h[p]; maxq=h[q]; if maxp<1e-9 or maxq<1e-9: continue
       # choose delta
       r=rng.random()
       if r < 0.4:
          delta = min(maxp,maxq) # full
       elif r < 0.8:
          delta = min(maxp,maxq) * rng.uniform(0.05,0.5)
       else:
          delta = min(maxp,maxq) * rng.uniform(0.005,0.05)
       if delta < 1e-8: continue
       apply_exchange(h,D,p,q,delta)
       nm,nc=get_max_info(D)
       diff=nm-cur_m
       accept=False
       if diff < -1e-10: accept=True
       elif abs(diff) <=1e-10:
          if nc < cur_cnt: accept=True
          elif rng.random() < 0.1: accept=True
       else:
          if diff < 1.0:
             if rng.random() < math.exp(-diff/max(T,1e-12)): accept=True
       if accept:
          cur_m=nm;cur_cnt=nc
          if nm < best_m -1e-10: best_m=nm; best_h=h.copy()
          no_imp=0
          # lists stale; continue, rebuild soon
       else:
          apply_exchange(h,D,q,p,delta)
          no_imp +=1
       trial +=1
       if trial % 1000 ==0:
          T=max(0.01, T*0.99)
          if no_imp > 20000:
             T=min(1.0,T+0.2); no_imp=0
    return cur_m,best_m,best_h
```
But if we modify lists only by rebuild, old p/q may become infeasible; we check. Rebuild every 2000 O(n). For n=256 okay.

```
def coordinate_change(h, D, p, delta):
    off = D.size//2
    src = off - p
    tgt = off + p - (D.size//2 - 1?) Wait off = n-1, n=D.size//2
```

```
def compute_c5(h):
   n=len(h); dx=2.0/n
   corr=np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full')
   return float(np.max(corr)*dx)
```

`targeted_continuous_improve(h,D,n,cur_m,cur_cnt,rng,attempts=200)`:
- Find up to 3 worst indices w where D near max.
- For each w, t = w - off. We need sample bad pairs along that lag with positive contribution. For t>0: valid i=0..n-t-1; contribution = h[i]*(1-h[i+t]). We can sample random i in range and if contribution > 0.001 candidate (p=i+t, q=i). For t<0: s=-t, valid i=s..n-1; contribution = h[i]*(1-h[i-s]); candidate p=i-s, q=i.
- For each candidate, evaluate exchange p inc q dec with delta = min(h[q], 1-h[p]) * u. u choices: full, 0.5, 0.1. Use `eval_swap_revert_continuous(h,D,p,q,delta,n)`:
   if p==q or delta<=0 skip
   apply_exchange(h,D,p,q,delta)
   nm,nc=get_max_info(D)
   apply_exchange(h,D,q,p,delta) # revert: increase q, decrease p by same delta
   return nm,nc
- Track best. After candidates, apply best if improves current. Return updated cur.
This function uses h values not necessarily binary. If delta full, may move to boundary.
Number candidates: worstlags 3 * sample 50 *3 deltas =450 evals; each eval two coordinate change (4 slice ops) + info + revert = maybe 0.5ms; 0.2s per call. If called rarely okay. Could reduce to 150.
We'll call in continuous phase when no_improve > 10000 and time > 5.

For binary phase targeted function can be separate or we could use continuous targeted with h binary; but binary targeted uses lists faster. We'll implement both? Code length grows. Maybe implement one generic `targeted_improve(h,D,n,cur_m,cur_cnt,rng, is_binary=False, ones=None,zeros=None)`. It can handle binary with lists or continuous without. To keep code simpler, implement continuous targeted and use it for both (for binary, p/q zero/one, delta full min =1). It doesn't use lists; it samples bad pairs by scanning random indices in the lag range (O(n)) and evaluates. That may be a bit slower but acceptable. Then we don't need binary-specific targeted. def targeted_improve(h,D,n,cur_m,cur_cnt,rng,eval_limit=150):
```
    off=n-1; maxD=D.max()
    worst_all=np.where(np.abs(D-maxD)<1e-10)[0]
    if worst_all.size==0: return ...
    wlist = worst_all if size<=3 else rng.choice(...)
    best_nm=cur_m; best_nc=cur_cnt; best_p=best_q=best_delta=0; found=False
    evaluated=0
    for w in wlist:
      t=w-off
      if t>0:
         low=0; high=n-t
         for _ in range(50):
            i=rng.randint(low, high) # high exclusive
            contrib=h[i]*(1-h[i+t])
            if contrib>1e-6:
               p=i+t; q=i; delta_max=min(h[q],1-h[p]); if delta_max>1e-9:
                  for u in (1.0,0.5,0.1): eval...
      elif t<0:
         s=-t; low=s; high=n
         for _ in range(50):
            i=rng.randint(low, high)
            contrib=h[i]*(1-h[i-s])
            if contrib>1e-6:
               p=i-s; q=i; ...
      else: # D0
         # D0 = sum h(1-h); reduce by making values more binary: choose p with high h(1-h) to decrease? Exchange? For D0, reducing requires moving h away from 0.5. Candidate: p where h<0.5 increase to1, q where h>0.5 decrease to0; but D0 exchange may. We can handle by random interior pairs.
         for _ in range(50): choose p=i with h<0.5? q=...
      if evaluated >= eval_limit: break
    if found: apply best; return best_nm,best_nc,True
    return cur_m,cur_cnt,False
```
Eval helper:

```
def eval_exchange_revert(h,D,p,q,delta):
    if p==q or delta <= 1e-12: return None
    # ensure feasibility with current h (since state restored between evals)
    if h[p] + delta > 1+1e-12 or h[q] - delta < -1e-12: return current? skip
    apply_exchange(h,D,p,q,delta)
    nm,nc=get_max_info(D)
    apply_exchange(h,D,q,p,delta)
    return nm,nc
```

Need be mindful `get_max_info(D)` returning count of near max; for D length maybe 0? n≥2.
```
def get_max_info(D):
    m = float(np.max(D))
    # Use tolerance relative
    tol = 1e-10
    cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(np.abs(D - m) <= tol))
    return m, cnt
```
For integer, tol okay. For continuous, count near max may count many if flat; that's fine. Could tol=1e-8. 
Potential performance: np.abs temp. Could do `cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(D >= m - tol))` since values not above max. This avoids abs. Use `D >= m - tol`. Good.

```
def targeted_improve(h,D,n,cur_m,cur_cnt,rng,eval_limit=150):
    off=n-1
    maxD=float(np.max(D))
    near = np.where(D >= maxD - 1e-10)[0]
    if near.size ==0: ...
    if near.size >3:
       wlist = rng.choice(near,3,replace=False)
    else: wlist=near
    best_nm, best_nc = cur_m, cur_cnt
    found=False; best_p=-1; best_q=-1; best_delta=0.0
    evaluated=0
    for w in wlist:
       t=int(w-off)
       if t >0:
          low=0; high=n-t
          if high<=low: continue
          for _ in range(60):
             i=int(rng.randint(low, high))
             contrib = h[i]*(1.0-h[i+t])
             if contrib > 1e-8:
                p=i+t; q=i; dmax=min(h[q],1.0-h[p])
                if dmax > 1e-9:
                   for u in (1.0,0.5,0.1):
                       delta=dmax*u
                       info=eval_exchange_revert(h,D,p,q,delta)
                       if info:
                          evaluated +=1
                          nm,nc=info
                          if nm < best_nm -1e-10 or (abs(nm-best_nm)<=1e-10 and nc<best_nc):
                              best_nm,best_nc=nm,nc; best_p,best_q,best_delta=p,q,delta; found=True
                       if evaluated >= eval_limit: break
          if evaluated >= eval_limit: break
       elif t <0:
          s=-t; low=s; high=n
          for _ in range(60):
             i=int(rng.randint(low, high))
             contrib = h[i]*(1.0-h[i-s])
             if contrib > 1e-8:
                p=i-s; q=i; dmax=min(h[q],1.0-h[p])...
       else:
          # D0; choose pairs pushing to boundaries
          for _ in range(60):
             p=int(rng.randint(n)); q=int(rng.randint(n))
             if p==q: continue
             # if h[p]<0.5 and h[q]>0.5 or vice versa, exchange to boundaries
             if h[p] < 0.5 and h[q] > 0.5:
                delta = min(1.0-h[p], h[q])
             elif h[p] > 0.5 and h[q] < 0.5:
                # To reduce D0, decreasing p and increasing q? p>0.5 should decrease, q<0.5 increase; exchange target q, source p => p_source=q? Our apply_exchange increases p inc, q dec. Let inc index be the low one, dec index high one.
                delta = min(1.0-h[q], h[p])
                p, q = q, p
                delta = min(1.0-h[q]? careful)
             else: continue
             if delta > 1e-6: eval...
```

`eval_exchange_revert`: Because it will be called many, function call overhead important but targeted only. Implement robust:
```
def eval_exchange_revert(h,D,p,q,delta):
    if p == q or delta <= 1e-14: return None
    if h[p] + delta > 1.0 + 1e-9 or h[q] - delta < -1e-9: return None
    oldp = h[p]; oldq = h[q]
    apply_exchange(h,D,p,q,delta)
    nm,nc = get_max_info(D)
    apply_exchange(h,D,q,p,delta)
    h[p] = oldp; h[q] = oldq
    return nm,nc
```
After reverse, h restored exactly. D may tiny residual. Could if D residual significant? No. If we want clean D, not.
But after reverse, D may not exactly original due float; repeating many evals could drift. We can after each candidate optionally if evaluated % 1000? Too many. But targeted eval limit small. Drift negligible. For continuous main loop rejections, we also restore h old but D drift. Recalc every 20000.

Main `optimize_state` binary phase code detail. Need avoid nested functions. We'll write inline.

Pseudo optimize_state:
```
def optimize_state(h_init, rng, timeout):
    h = np.array(h_init, dtype=float).copy()
    n = int(h.size)
    h = enforce_sum(h, n/2.0)
    D = initial_d(h)
    cur_m, cur_cnt = get_max_info(D)
    best_m = cur_m; best_cnt=cur_cnt; best_h = h.copy()
    start=time.time(); deadline=start+timeout
    # binary if close
    hr = np.round(h)
    if np.max(np.abs(h - hr)) < 1e-7:
       h = hr
       D = initial_d(h)
       cur_m,cur_cnt=get_max_info(D)
       best_m=cur_m; best_h=h.copy()
       zeros = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] < 0.5]
       ones = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] >= 0.5]
       T=1.5; no_improve=0; trial=0; next_check=2000
       while True:
          if trial == next_check:
             if time.time() >= deadline: break
             next_check = trial + 2000
          if len(zeros)==0 or len(ones)==0: break
          pi = rng.randint(len(zeros)); oi = rng.randint(len(ones))
          p = zeros[pi]; q = ones[oi]
          apply_exchange(h,D,p,q,1.0)
          nm,nc = get_max_info(D)
          diff = nm - cur_m
          accept=False
          if diff < -1e-12: accept=True
          elif abs(diff) <= 1e-12:
              if nc < cur_cnt: accept=True
              elif rng.random() < 0.02: accept=True
          else:
              if diff <= 4.0:
                  if rng.random() < math.exp(-diff / max(T,1e-12)): accept=True
          if accept:
              cur_m=nm; cur_cnt=nc
              if nm < best_m - 1e-12:
                  best_m=nm; best_h=h.copy()
              zeros[pi]=q; ones[oi]=p
              no_improve=0
          else:
              apply_exchange(h,D,q,p,1.0)
              no_improve += 1
          trial += 1
          if trial % 1000 == 0:
              T = max(0.05, T * 0.99)
              if no_improve > 8000:
                  # targeted
                  if time.time() < deadline - 30:
                      newm,newc,acc = targeted_improve(h,D,n,cur_m,cur_cnt,rng,eval_limit=200)
                      if acc:
                         cur_m,cur_cnt = newm,newc
                         if cur_m < best_m - 1e-12:
                            best_m=cur_m; best_h=h.copy()
                         # rebuild lists
                         zeros = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] < 0.5]
                         ones = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] >= 0.5]
                      no_improve = 0
                  else:
                      no_improve = 0
              if no_improve > 50000:
                  T = min(3.0, T + 1.0)
                  no_improve = 0
       # after binary
       # reset to best for continuous
       if time.time() < deadline - 20:
           h = best_h.copy()
           D = initial_d(h)
           cur_m = best_m
           cur_cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(D >= cur_m - 1e-10))
```
But note `targeted_improve` may accept an improvement to current state but not necessarily less than best? It returns current improved. It modifies h/D. We then rebuild lists. Good.
If targeted called during binary phase, it may use continuous deltas (0.1,0.5) from binary h; full delta 1 plus 0.5 partial make h fractional! That's okay; but zeros/ones rebuild will include fractional? We define zeros h<0.5, ones h>=0.5. If 0.5, ambiguous; binary phase assumes h binary-ish but after 0.5 partial, h may be .5 values. The binary swap with p from zeros h<0.5 and q ones h>=0.5 using delta=1 may be infeasible (if p=0.4 +1 >1? no p<0.5, max p maybe .5; adding 1 to .4 ->1.4 infeasible!). Ah, binary phase with list assumes zeros are exactly 0 and ones exactly1. If targeted partial changes, binary loop breaks. So in binary phase targeted should only use full delta (u=1) to maintain binary. Or after targeted with partial, switch to continuous. We can make `targeted_improve` parameter `max_delta=1.0, binary_only=False`? Simpler: in binary phase, call `targeted_improve` with `allowed_us=(1.0,)`? But our function top-level can't take variable length easily? It can take `binary_flag` and use only u=1. Or we can avoid targeted in binary phase and rely random + anneal; but targeted useful. Add parameter `targeted_improve(h,D,n,cur_m,cur_cnt,rng,eval_limit=150,binary_only=False)`. If binary_only, only u=1.0 and skip D0? OK.
Then binary lists remain valid. Rebuild lists using exact 0/1 (should). Good.
```
if binary_only: for u in (1.0,) else (1.0,0.5,0.1)
```
Also in binary_only, candidate source/target must be exactly 1/0? We sample contributions >1e-6; if h binary, good. If somehow fractional not, apply_exchange delta=1 feasibility might fail; eval_exchange_revert checks. OK.

Continuous phase code inside optimize_state:
```
if time.time() < deadline - 10:
    h = best_h.copy()
    D = initial_d(h)
    cur_m = best_m; cur_cnt=...
    T_big=0.5; T_small=0.03; no_imp=0; trial=0; next_check=2000
    # build lists
    inc = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] < 1.0 - 1e-9]
    dec = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] > 1e-9]
    rebuild_count=0
    while True:
       if trial == next_check:
          if time.time() >= deadline: break
          next_check = trial+2000
       if not inc or not dec:
          inc=...; dec=...
          if not: break
       # rebuild every 3000
       if trial % 3000 ==0:
          inc=...; dec=...
       p=inc[rng.randint(len(inc))]; q=dec[rng.randint(len(dec))]; if p==q: continue
       maxd=min(1-h[p], h[q])
       if maxd < 1e-10: continue
       r=rng.random()
       if r < 0.35: delta=maxd
       elif r<0.75: delta=maxd*rng.uniform(0.05,0.5)
       else: delta=maxd*rng.uniform(0.005,0.05)
       if delta < 1e-8: continue
       oldp=h[p]; oldq=h[q]
       apply_exchange(h,D,p,q,delta)
       nm,nc=get_max_info(D)
       diff=nm-cur_m
       accept=False
       if diff < -1e-10: accept=True
       elif abs(diff)<=1e-10:
           if nc<cur_cnt: accept=True
           elif rng.random()<0.05: accept=True
       else:
           if delta >= 0.5:
              if diff <= 1.0 and rng.random() < math.exp(-diff/max(T_big,1e-12)): accept=True
           else:
              if diff <= 0.25 and rng.random() < math.exp(-diff/max(T_small,1e-12)): accept=True
       if accept:
           cur_m=nm; cur_cnt=nc
           if nm < best_m - 1e-12:
              best_m=nm; best_h=h.copy()
           no_imp=0
       else:
           apply_exchange(h,D,q,p,delta)
           h[p]=oldp; h[q]=oldq
           no_imp+=1
       trial +=1
       if trial % 2000 ==0:
          T_big=max(0.05,T_big*0.99); T_small=max(0.001,T_small*0.99)
          if no_imp > 8000 and time.time() < deadline-30:
             newm,newc,acc=targeted_improve(h,D,n,cur_m,cur_cnt,rng,eval_limit=150,binary_only=False)
             if acc:
                cur_m,cur_cnt=newm,newc
                if cur_m < best_m -1e-12: best_m=cur_m; best_h=h.copy()
             no_imp=0
          if cur_m > best_m + 2.0 and no_imp > 50000:
             h=best_h.copy(); D=initial_d(h); cur_m=best_m; cur_cnt=...; inc/dec rebuild; no_imp=0; trial=0
       if trial % 50000 ==0:
          D[:] = initial_d(h)
          cur_m,cur_cnt=get_max_info(D)
          # don't reset best if current worse
```

```
import numpy as np
import math
import time

def compute_c5(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = h.size
    if n == 0: return 0.0
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    corr = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')
    return float(np.max(corr) * dx)
```

`enforce_sum`:
```
def enforce_sum(h, target):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float, copy=True)
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    # if n=0
    for _ in range(200):
        s = float(np.sum(h))
        diff = float(target) - s
        if abs(diff) <= 1e-14:
            break
        if diff > 0:
            room = 1.0 - h
            idx = int(np.argmax(room))
            add = min(float(room[idx]), diff)
            h[idx] += add
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(h))
            sub = min(float(h[idx]), -diff)
            h[idx] -= sub
    # distribute tiny
    s = float(np.sum(h))
    diff = float(target) - s
    if abs(diff) > 1e-12:
        if diff > 0:
            mask = h < 1.0 - 1e-12
        else:
            mask = h > 1e-12
        cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
        if cnt > 0:
            h[mask] += diff / float(cnt)
            h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    return h
```
For target not exactly sum of floats, okay.

`initial_d`:
```
def initial_d(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = h.size
    D = np.zeros(2*n-1, dtype=float)
    if n == 0: return D
    off = n-1
    omh = 1.0 - h
    D[off] = float(np.dot(h, omh))
    for t in range(1, n):
        D[off + t] = float(np.dot(h[:n-t], omh[t:]))
        D[off - t] = float(np.dot(h[t:], omh[:n-t]))
    return D
```

`get_max_info`:
```
def get_max_info(D):
    m = float(np.max(D))
    tol = 1e-10
    cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(D >= m - tol))
    return m, cnt
```

`coordinate_change`:
```
def coordinate_change(h, D, p, delta):
    n = h.size
    off = n - 1
    if delta == 0.0: return
    src = off - p
    # src >=0? p<=n-1, off-p >=0; src+n = off-p+n = 2n-1-p <=2n-1? yes end exclusive within D length? If p=0, src=n-1, end=2n-1 length 2n-1. If p=n-1, src=0,end=n-1? Wait src+n=2n-1-p = n? For p=n-1, src=0,n? D length 2n-1 indices 0..2n-2; src+n = n? n <=2n-1, OK.
    D[src:src+n] += delta * (1.0 - h)
    tgt = off + p - off  # off + p - (n-1)? off=n-1, so tgt = p? Wait earlier target start offset + p - (n-1) = p. But offset = n-1, so off + p - (n-1) = p. Is that correct? Let's check n=4,p=0 target start offset+p-(n-1)= off - off=0 -> p=0. p=3 -> start=3? In earlier examples n=2,p=1 target start=1, p=1. So tgt = p. For n=5,p=2 target start=2? Since offset=4, 4+2-4=2. Yes target slice start index equals p! Nice.
    D[p:p+n] -= delta * h[::-1]
    D[off] -= delta * delta
    h[p] += delta
```
Wow target slice start is p, not `off + p - off` yes p. Source start off - p. Good.
Check p=n-1 target: D[n-1:2n-1] length n, within D length 2n-1? end=2n-1 exclusive, indices n-1..2n-2 good. p=0 target D[0:n] indices 0..n-1. Source p=n-1 D[0:n], p=0 source D[n-1:2n-1]. Symmetric.
This simplifies.
Need include D0 adjustment: D[off] subtract δ². The source and target slices both include off. OK.

Let's verify D target orientation with p formula using reversed h: for target slice start p, as idx k=0..n-1, t=off+p? Wait D index idx = p+k. We need t = idx-off = p+k-off. Target index a = p - t = p - (p+k-off) = off - k = n-1-k. So h[a]=h[n-1-k]=h[::-1][k]. Yes.

`apply_exchange`:
```
def apply_exchange(h,D,p,q,delta):
    if p == q or delta == 0.0: return
    coordinate_change(h,D,p,delta)
    coordinate_change(h,D,q,-delta)
```
Because increasing p by δ, decreasing q by δ. coordinate_change will update h sequentially.

`eval_exchange_revert`:
```
def eval_exchange_revert(h,D,p,q,delta):
    if p == q or delta <= 1e-15: return None
    if h[p] + delta > 1.0 + 1e-10 or h[q] - delta < -1e-10:
        return None
    oldp = float(h[p]); oldq = float(h[q])
    apply_exchange(h,D,p,q,delta)
    nm,nc = get_max_info(D)
    apply_exchange(h,D,q,p,delta)
    h[p] = oldp; h[q] = oldq
    return nm, nc
```
After reverse, D maybe not exact but h restored. If D drift, repeated. OK.

`targeted_improve`:
```
def targeted_improve(h,D,n,cur_m,cur_cnt,rng,eval_limit=150,binary_only=False):
    off=n-1
    maxD = float(np.max(D))
    near = np.where(D >= maxD - 1e-10)[0]
    if near.size == 0: return cur_m,cur_cnt,False
    if near.size > 3:
        wlist = rng.choice(near, 3, replace=False)
    else:
        wlist = near
    best_nm = cur_m; best_nc = cur_cnt
    best_p=-1; best_q=-1; best_delta=0.0; found=False; evaluated=0
    if binary_only:
        us=(1.0,)
    else:
        us=(1.0,0.5,0.1)
    for w in wlist:
        t = int(w - off)
        if evaluated >= eval_limit: break
        if t > 0:
            low=0; high=n-t
            if high <= low: continue
            for _ in range(60):
                i = int(rng.randint(low, high))
                contrib = h[i] * (1.0 - h[i+t])
                if contrib > 1e-8:
                    p = i+t; q = i; dmax = min(float(h[q]), float(1.0 - h[p]))
                    if dmax > 1e-9:
                        for u in us:
                            delta = dmax * u
                            info = eval_exchange_revert(h,D,p,q,delta)
                            if info is not None:
                                evaluated += 1
                                nm,nc=info
                                if nm < best_nm - 1e-10 or (abs(nm - best_nm) <= 1e-10 and nc < best_nc):
                                    best_nm,best_nc,best_p,best_q,best_delta=nm,nc,p,q,delta; found=True
                            if evaluated >= eval_limit: break
                if evaluated >= eval_limit: break
        elif t < 0:
            s = -t; low=s; high=n
            for _ in range(60):
                i = int(rng.randint(low, high))
                contrib = h[i] * (1.0 - h[i-s])
                if contrib > 1e-8:
                    p = i-s; q = i; dmax = min(float(h[q]), float(1.0 - h[p]))
                    if dmax > 1e-9:
                        for u in us:
                            delta = dmax * u
                            info = eval_exchange_revert(h,D,p,q,delta)
                            if info is not None:
                                evaluated +=1
                                nm,nc=info
                                if nm < best_nm -1e-10 or (abs(nm-best_nm)<=1e-10 and nc<best_nc):
                                    best_nm,best_nc,best_p,best_q,best_delta=nm,nc,p,q,delta; found=True
                            if evaluated >= eval_limit: break
                if evaluated >= eval_limit: break
        else:
            # D0: push low higher and high lower? Actually inc low, dec high.
            for _ in range(60):
                p = int(rng.randint(n)); q = int(rng.randint(n))
                if p == q: continue
                if h[p] < 0.5 and h[q] > 0.5:
                    dmax = min(1.0 - h[p], h[q])
                elif h[p] > 0.5 and h[q] < 0.5:
                    p2,q2 = q,p
                    dmax = min(1.0 - h[p2], h[q2])
                    p,q = p2,q2
                else:
                    continue
                if dmax > 1e-9:
                    for u in us:
                        delta = dmax * u
                        info = eval_exchange_revert(h,D,p,q,delta)
                        if info is not None:
                            evaluated +=1
                            nm,nc=info
                            if nm < best_nm -1e-10 or (abs(nm-best_nm)<=1e-10 and nc<best_nc):
                                best_nm,best_nc,best_p,best_q,best_delta=nm,nc,p,q,delta; found=True
                        if evaluated >= eval_limit: break
                if evaluated >= eval_limit: break
    if found:
        apply_exchange(h,D,best_p,best_q,best_delta)
    return best_nm,best_nc,found
```
Potential issue: In D0 branch, if p high, q low, we swap p,q and dmax computed. Need ensure p is low after. Good.

`thue_morse_h`:
```
def thue_morse_h(n):
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    for i in range(n):
        if bin(i).count('1') % 2 == 0:
            h[i]=1.0
    return h
```
For n not power, might not balanced; but we use powers. If n=1024. If parent n odd? not.

`random_binary`:
```
def random_binary(n, rng):
    h=np.zeros(n,dtype=float)
    target=n//2
    if target>0:
       idx=rng.choice(n,target,replace=False)
       h[idx]=1.0
    return h
```

`resample_values`:
```
def resample_values(vals, n):
    vals=np.asarray(vals,dtype=float)
    m=vals.size
    if m == n:
       return vals.copy()
    if m == 1:
       return np.full(n, float(vals[0]))
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,m,endpoint=False)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n,endpoint=False)
    return np.interp(x_new,x_old,vals)
```

`threshold_top_half`:
```
def threshold_top_half(vals, n, rng=None, noise=0.0):
    v = np.asarray(vals, dtype=float).copy()
    v = np.clip(v,0.0,1.0)
    if noise > 0 and rng is not None:
        v = v + rng.normal(0.0, noise, v.size)
    target = n // 2
    if target <=0:
        return np.zeros(n)
    if target >= n:
        return np.ones(n)
    order = np.argsort(v, kind='stable')[::-1]
    idx = order[:target]
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    h[idx] = 1.0
    return h
```
If v after noise not within [0,1], sorting fine. h balanced.

`antisym_from_first`:
```
def antisym_from_first(first):
    first = np.asarray(first,dtype=float).reshape(-1)
    m=first.size
    return np.concatenate((first, 1.0-first))
```
Length 2m. For n=256, first=128. Sum = m. Good.

`process_tasks`:
```
def process_tasks(tasks, best_h, best_c5, best_n, rng, start_time, budget_s):
    reserve = 10.0
    for task in tasks:
       if len(task) !=3: continue
       n,h_init,timeout = task
       rem = float(budget_s) - (time.time() - start_time)
       if rem < 20.0: break
       timeout = min(float(timeout), rem - reserve)
       if timeout < 10.0: break
       h_opt, m_opt = optimize_state(h_init, rng, timeout)
       # candidate 1
       h_cand = enforce_sum(h_opt, n/2.0)
       c_cand = compute_c5(h_cand)
       # rounded candidate
       hr = np.round(h_opt)
       if np.max(np.abs(h_opt - hr)) < 0.1:
           h_r = enforce_sum(hr, n/2.0)
           c_r = compute_c5(h_r)
           if c_r < c_cand:
               h_cand = h_r; c_cand = c_r
       if c_cand < best_c5 - 1e-12:
           best_h = h_cand.copy(); best_c5 = float(c_cand); best_n = int(n)
       rem = float(budget_s) - (time.time() - start_time)
       if rem < 20.0: break
    return best_h,best_c5,best_n
```
If `optimize_state` returns h_opt maybe best_h from inside; no issue.

`optimize_state` code carefully. Need to pass `rng` which is RandomState; when we use `rng.choice` in targeted, okay. Need not reseed.

```
def optimize_state(h_init, rng, timeout):
    h = np.asarray(h_init, dtype=float).copy()
    n = int(h.size)
    h = enforce_sum(h, n/2.0)
    D = initial_d(h)
    cur_m, cur_cnt = get_max_info(D)
    best_m = cur_m
    best_h = h.copy()
    start = time.time()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(timeout))
    # binary phase
    hr = np.round(h)
    if float(np.max(np.abs(h - hr))) < 1e-7:
        h = hr
        D = initial_d(h)
        cur_m, cur_cnt = get_max_info(D)
        best_m = cur_m
        best_h = h.copy()
        zeros = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] < 0.5]
        ones = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] >= 0.5]
        T = 1.5
        no_improve = 0
        trial = 0
        next_check = 2000
        while True:
            if trial == next_check:
                if time.time() >= deadline:
                    break
                next_check = trial + 2000
            if zeros and ones:
                pi = rng.randint(len(zeros)); oi = rng.randint(len(ones))
                p = zeros[pi]; q = ones[oi]
                apply_exchange(h,D,p,q,1.0)
                nm,nc = get_max_info(D)
                diff = nm - cur_m
                accept = False
                improved = False
                if diff < -1e-12:
                    accept = True; improved = True
                elif abs(diff) <= 1e-12:
                    if nc < cur_cnt:
                        accept = True; improved = True
                    elif rng.random() < 0.02:
                        accept = True
                else:
                    if diff <= 4.0:
                        prob = math.exp(-diff / max(T, 1e-12))
                        if rng.random() < prob:
                            accept = True
                if accept:
                    cur_m = nm; cur_cnt = nc
                    if nm < best_m - 1e-12:
                        best_m = nm; best_h = h.copy()
                    zeros[pi] = q; ones[oi] = p
                    if improved:
                        no_improve = 0
                    else:
                        no_improve += 1
                else:
                    apply_exchange(h,D,q,p,1.0)
                    no_improve += 1
            trial += 1
            if trial % 1000 == 0:
                T = max(0.05, T * 0.99)
                if no_improve > 8000 and time.time() < deadline - 30.0:
                    newm,newc,acc = targeted_improve(h,D,n,cur_m,cur_cnt,rng,eval_limit=200,binary_only=True)
                    if acc:
                        cur_m,cur_cnt = newm,newc
                        if cur_m < best_m - 1e-12:
                            best_m = cur_m; best_h = h.copy()
                        zeros = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] < 0.5]
                        ones = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] >= 0.5]
                    no_improve = 0
                if no_improve > 50000:
                    T = min(3.0, T + 1.0)
                    no_improve = 0
            if time.time() >= deadline:
                break
        # after binary phase reset to best for continuous
        if time.time() < deadline - 15.0:
            h = best_h.copy()
            D = initial_d(h)
            cur_m = best_m
            cur_cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(D >= cur_m - 1e-10))
    # continuous phase
    if time.time() < deadline - 5.0:
        # ensure h best
        h = best_h.copy()
        D = initial_d(h)
        cur_m = best_m
        cur_cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(D >= cur_m - 1e-10))
        T_big = 0.5
        T_small = 0.03
        no_improve = 0
        trial = 0
        next_check = 2000
        inc = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] < 1.0 - 1e-9]
        dec = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] > 1e-9]
        while True:
            if trial == next_check:
                if time.time() >= deadline:
                    break
                next_check = trial + 2000
            if not inc or not dec:
                inc = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] < 1.0 - 1e-9]
                dec = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] > 1e-9]
                if not inc or not dec:
                    break
            if trial % 3000 == 0:
                inc = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] < 1.0 - 1e-9]
                dec = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] > 1e-9]
                if not inc or not dec:
                    break
            p = inc[rng.randint(len(inc))]
            q = dec[rng.randint(len(dec))]
            if p != q:
                maxd = min(1.0 - h[p], h[q])
                if maxd > 1e-10:
                    r = rng.random()
                    if r < 0.35:
                        delta = maxd
                    elif r < 0.75:
                        delta = maxd * rng.uniform(0.05, 0.5)
                    else:
                        delta = maxd * rng.uniform(0.005, 0.05)
                    if delta > 1e-9:
                        oldp = float(h[p]); oldq = float(h[q])
                        apply_exchange(h,D,p,q,delta)
                        nm,nc = get_max_info(D)
                        diff = nm - cur_m
                        accept = False
                        improved = False
                        if diff < -1e-10:
                            accept = True; improved = True
                        elif abs(diff) <= 1e-10:
                            if nc < cur_cnt:
                                accept = True; improved = True
                            elif rng.random() < 0.05:
                                accept = True
                        else:
                            if delta >= 0.5:
                                if diff <= 1.0 and rng.random() < math.exp(-diff / max(T_big,1e-12)):
                                    accept = True
                            else:
                                if diff <= 0.25 and rng.random() < math.exp(-diff / max(T_small,1e-12)):
                                    accept = True
                        if accept:
                            cur_m=nm; cur_cnt=nc
                            if nm < best_m - 1e-12:
                                best_m=nm; best_h=h.copy()
                            if improved:
                                no_improve=0
                            else:
                                no_improve += 1
                        else:
                            apply_exchange(h,D,q,p,delta)
                            h[p]=oldp; h[q]=oldq
                            no_improve += 1
            trial += 1
            if trial % 2000 == 0:
                T_big = max(0.05, T_big * 0.99)
                T_small = max(0.001, T_small * 0.99)
                if no_improve > 8000 and time.time() < deadline - 30.0:
                    newm,newc,acc = targeted_improve(h,D,n,cur_m,cur_cnt,rng,eval_limit=150,binary_only=False)
                    if acc:
                        cur_m,cur_cnt = newm,newc
                        if cur_m < best_m - 1e-12:
                            best_m = cur_m; best_h = h.copy()
                    no_improve = 0
                if cur_m > best_m + 2.0 and no_improve > 50000 and time.time() < deadline - 30.0:
                    h = best_h.copy()
                    D = initial_d(h)
                    cur_m = best_m
                    cur_cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(D >= cur_m - 1e-10))
                    inc = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] < 1.0 - 1e-9]
                    dec = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] > 1e-9]
                    no_improve = 0
                if trial % 50000 == 0:
                    D_new = initial_d(h)
                    D[:] = D_new
                    cur_m,cur_cnt = get_max_info(D)
            if time.time() >= deadline:
                break
    return best_h, best_m
```

```
def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    np.random.seed(seed)
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    start_time = time.time()
    best_h=None; best_c5=float('inf'); best_n=0
    parent=None
    try:
        pv = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float)
        if pv.size > 0:
            parent = pv.copy()
    except Exception:
        parent = None
    if parent is not None:
        hp = enforce_sum(parent, parent.size/2.0)
        c = compute_c5(hp)
        best_h=hp; best_c5=c; best_n=int(parent.size)
    # Stage 256

        hp = resample_values(parent, n)

        tasks.append((n, enforce_sum(hp, n/2.0), 100))
        tasks.append((n, threshold_top_half(hp, n, rng, 0.03), 60))
    else:
        tasks.append((n, random_binary(n,rng), 150))
    tasks.append((n, thue_morse_h(n), 80))
    tasks.append((n, antisym_from_first(thue_morse_h(n//2)), 40))
    tasks.append((n, random_binary(n,rng), 50))
```
